1. python-multipart: Python-Multipart: Denial of Service via crafted form-urlencoded bodies

Problem

The project’s requirements.txt pins python-multipart at version 0.0.20, which is known to contain CVE‑2026‑53539. The CVE describes a denial‑of‑service condition triggered by specially crafted form‑urlencoded bodies. No version‑upgrade or mitigation is present in the supplied dependency list, and the scanner evidence is of high severity, providing independent corroboration of the vulnerability.

Impact

An attacker can send a maliciously crafted request that causes the application to consume excessive resources or crash, leading to denial of service for legitimate users.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-53539

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-53539 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.30.

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.30 or later, which contains the fix for CVE‑2026‑53539. Update the requirements.txt accordingly and redeploy the application.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner trivy high at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-53539

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-051697f9b8567d596874

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

2. A note must belong to the order named in the URL

Problem

The endpoint /orders/{order_id}/notes/{note_id} retrieves a note by its ID without verifying that the note's order_id matches the order_id supplied in the URL. The code executes a query SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s and returns the row if found, raising a 404 only when the note does not exist. No check is performed to ensure the note belongs to the requested order, allowing an attacker to access notes belonging to other orders by guessing note IDs. Runtime probing confirms the flaw: a GET request to /orders/1/notes/2 returned HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403/404, demonstrating unauthorized access.

Impact

An attacker can enumerate or read notes belonging to other users' orders, leading to confidentiality breaches and potential exposure of sensitive information across accounts.

Location

/orders/1/notes/2

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source code shows note fetched without order_id check (lines 543‑549).
  • Runtime probe fact: GET /orders/1/notes/2 returned status 200, contradicting expected 403/404.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.nested_note.1 · target /orders/1/notes/2 · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: GET /orders/1/notes/2violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output

assertion_resultsfail
expected_statuses403, 404
methodGET
status200
step1
values_redactedyes
workflow_idnested_note
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Add a validation step that compares the order_id of the retrieved note with the order_id path parameter. If they differ, return a 403 Forbidden or 404 Not Found. Example:
``python
row = conn.execute("SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s", (note_id,)).fetchone()
if not row or row["order_id"] != order_id:
raise HTTPException(404, "Note not found")
return dict(row)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/1/notes/2:? rules=1

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

3. Job listing requires admin role

Problem

The endpoint /admin/jobs calls principal(authorization) but does not enforce an admin role check before returning job data. The source excerpt shows no role verification, and the runtime probe fact records a GET request receiving HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403/404, confirming unrestricted access.

Impact

Unauthorized users can retrieve the full list of jobs, potentially exposing sensitive internal information and enabling further attacks.

Issue group merged-15dd977d52312c30635a

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

4. Insecure profile access allowing enumeration of other users' data

Problem

The endpoint /profiles/{profile_id} authenticates the request via principal(authorization) but does not verify that the authenticated user is the owner of the requested profile before querying the database. The SQL query returns the full user record (including sensitive fields) for any profile_id supplied. Runtime probing confirms that a request for another user's profile returns HTTP 200, whereas the expected behavior is a 403 or 404.

Impact

An attacker can retrieve arbitrary users' personal information (username, email, SSN) by iterating profile_id values, leading to privacy breaches and potential identity theft.

Location

/profiles/{{other_id}}

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 2

Evidence

  • Source code shows no ownership check before returning user data
  • Runtime probe fact: GET /profiles/{{other_id}} returned status 200 instead of expected 403/404

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.profile_owner.2 · target /profiles/{{other_id}} · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_b: GET /mepassed - HTTP 200, expected 200, checks 1/1 passed
  2. 2. user_a: GET /profiles/{{other_id}}violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output

assertion_resultsfail
expected_statuses403, 404
methodGET
status200
step2
values_redactedyes
workflow_idprofile_owner
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Add an ownership check after retrieving the row (or before the query) to ensure the authenticated user's ID matches profile_id. Return 403/404 when the user is not authorized. Example:
``python
user_id = get_user_id_from_token(authorization)
if user_id != profile_id:
raise HTTPException(403, "Forbidden")
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /profiles/{{other_id}}:? rules=2

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-18063ba27bc289f53c8a

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-89OWASP A03 Injection

5. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

Problem

The legacy_login endpoint constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided username and password into the query string using an f‑string. No parameterized query or input sanitization is performed, and there is no visible guard or validation step before the query is executed. This allows an attacker to inject arbitrary SQL via the username or password fields, leading to unauthorized data access or manipulation.

Impact

An attacker can perform SQL injection to retrieve, modify, or delete data from the users table, potentially compromising all user accounts and escalating privileges.

Location

api/extra.py:119

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • api/extra.py: line 117 builds query with f"SELECT id,username,role FROM users WHERE username='{body.username}' AND password='{body.password}'"
  • No input sanitization or parameterization is present before conn.execute(query)

Recommended fix

Replace the string interpolation with a parameterized query using the database library's placeholders (e.g., SELECT ... WHERE username=? AND password=?) and pass the user inputs as parameters. Additionally, hash passwords and compare hashes instead of storing plaintext passwords.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:119 rules=py/sql-injection

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-18f1abe978f9fdaad8e6

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

6. Payment replay vulnerability on order payment endpoint

Problem

The pay_order endpoint updates the order status to 'paid' without verifying whether the order has already been paid. The runtime probe confirms that a second POST to the same endpoint returns HTTP 200 instead of an error (expected 400/409/422), demonstrating that the endpoint allows replay attacks.

Impact

An attacker can submit multiple payment requests for the same order, causing duplicate charges or fraudulent invoicing.

Location

/orders/{{order_id}}/pay

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 3

Evidence

  • Source code shows pay_order updates status to 'paid' without prior status check (lines 242-250).
  • Runtime probe fact indicates POST to /orders/{{order_id}}/pay returned 200 instead of expected error codes, confirming replay is possible.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.payment_replay.3 · target /orders/{{order_id}}/pay · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: POST /orderspassed - HTTP 200, expected 200, 201, checks 1/1 passed
  2. 2. user_a: POST /orders/{{order_id}}/paypassed - HTTP 200, expected 200, checks 1/1 passed
  3. 3. user_a: POST /orders/{{order_id}}/payviolation - HTTP 200, expected 400, 409, 422, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output
actoruser_a
assertion_resultsfail
expected_statuses400, 409, 422
methodPOST
status200
step3
values_redactedyes
workflow_idpayment_replay
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Add a check for the order's current status before processing payment. If the status is already 'paid', return an appropriate error (e.g., 409 Conflict). Ensure the status check and update are performed atomically within a transaction.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/{{order_id}}/pay:? rules=3

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-1bcb1da144dffdcf3fd8

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-312CWE-359CWE-532

7. Clear-text logging of sensitive information

Problem

The code logs the application secret (APP_SECRET) in clear text via log.info("login user=%s app_secret=%s", username, os.environ.get("APP_SECRET", "lab-only")). No redaction or conditional guard is present before the logging statement, allowing the secret to be written to logs. The semgrep scanner flagged this line as a credential disclosure issue (rule python.lang.security.audit.logging.logger-credential-leak), providing independent corroboration of the vulnerability.

Impact

Exposing the application secret in logs can lead to credential leakage. An attacker with access to log files could retrieve the secret and potentially impersonate the application, access protected resources, or compromise the entire system.

Location

api/app.py:137

Detected by

  • codeql-cli + semgrep-ce: py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data, python-logger-credential-disclosure

Evidence

  • Source line 137 logs app_secret=%s with the value from os.environ.get("APP_SECRET", "lab-only")
  • Semgrep finding (python.lang.security.audit.logging.logger-credential-leak) flags the same line as a credential disclosure

Recommended fix

Remove the secret from the log statement or replace it with a placeholder. If logging is required for debugging, ensure that sensitive values are masked or omitted. Example:
``python
log.info("login user=%s", username) # Do not log APP_SECRET
``
Alternatively, use a dedicated secret‑masking logger or environment‑specific logging configuration that redacts secrets.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli,semgrep-ce high at api/app.py:137 rules=py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data|python-logger-credential-disclosure

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-3202e1e1074cfd44b9bf

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-606CWE-770

8. python-multipart: Denial of Service via excessive multipart part headers

Problem

No evidence of an upgrade or mitigation is present in the supplied files.

Impact

Issue group merged-d58491aad5be35b48233

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-79CWE-116OWASP A03 Injection

17. Reflected server-side cross-site scripting

Problem

The endpoint /catalog/search directly interpolates the user‑controlled query parameter q into an HTML response (<h1>Results for {q}</h1> and <p>{row['name']}</p>). No escaping or sanitisation is performed, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript that will be executed in the victim's browser when the page is rendered. The same unsanitised value is also used in a raw SQL string, indicating additional risk (SQL injection). The supplied source excerpt demonstrates the vulnerable code path, and the finding originates from a scanner, providing independent corroboration of the issue.

Impact

An attacker can execute arbitrary JavaScript in the context of the victim's browser, leading to session hijacking, credential theft, defacement, or further attacks against the user or the application.

Location

api/extra.py:145

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/reflective-xss

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows q interpolated into HTML without escaping (lines 142‑145).
  • Scanner‑generated finding flagged the endpoint as reflected XSS (high severity).

Recommended fix

Validate and sanitise the q parameter before using it in HTML output. Use a templating engine that auto‑escapes content or explicitly escape special characters (e.g., html.escape). Additionally, replace the string‑formatted SQL query with parameterised queries to prevent SQL injection. Example:
``python
@router.get("/catalog/search", response_class=HTMLResponse)
def catalog_search(q: str = ""):
with connection() as conn:
rows = conn.execute("SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s", (f"%{q}%",)).fetchall()
safe_q = html.escape(q)
return f"<h1>Results for {safe_q}</h1>" + "".join(f"<p>{html.escape(row['name'])}</p>" for row in rows)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:145 rules=py/reflective-xss

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Problem

The apply_coupon endpoint authenticates the request via principal(authorization) but does not perform any check that the authenticated user owns the order identified by order_id or possesses a privileged role before executing an UPDATE on the orders table. Impact

An attacker could reduce the price of any order, potentially causing revenue loss, unauthorized discounts, or abuse of promotional coupons. In a worst‑case scenario, this could be combined with other attacks to manipulate order totals or trigger downstream business logic that assumes the discount was authorized.

Location

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • 232: principal(authorization) – authentication occurs but no further checks
  • 236: row = conn.execute("UPDATE orders SET discount=%s WHERE id=%s RETURNING *", (percent, order_id)).fetchone() – updates order without ownership verification

Limitations

  • No visibility into the implementation of principal() to determine whether it returns role information that could be used for authorization checks.
  • No scanner or runtime probe evidence confirming that the missing check can be exploited in practice.

Recommended fix

Add an ownership or role verification step after authentication and before the UPDATE. For example, query the order to retrieve its owner and compare it to the principal’s user ID, or check that the principal has an admin/manager role. Abort with a 403 Forbidden if the check fails. Ensure that any future modifications to order data also enforce this check, possibly via a database trigger or service‑level policy.

Why this decision

raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Discount out of range")
percent = body.discount
else:
percent = coupon["percent"] if coupon else 0
``

41. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

Problem

The endpoint catalog_search constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided query parameter q into the statement using an f‑string. This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized, escaped, or parameterized before being sent to the database.

Impact

An attacker could supply crafted input (e.g., q=anything' OR '1'='1) to manipulate the SQL query, potentially retrieving all product names, extracting data, or executing arbitrary SQL commands depending on database permissions.

Location

api/extra.py:144

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt lines 141‑145 show the f‑string interpolation of q into the SQL query without sanitization.

Limitations

  • No independent runtime probe or additional scanner evidence confirming exploitation. A proof‑of‑concept request demonstrating the injection effect is not provided.

Recommended fix

Rewrite the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database driver, e.g., conn.execute("SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s", (f"%{q}%",)). Additionally, validate or whitelist allowed characters for q and consider limiting the length of the input.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:144 rules=py/sql-injection

42. Uncontrolled data used in path expression

Problem

The endpoint constructs a file path from a user‑provided name but immediately resolves it and checks file.is_relative_to(root) and file.is_file(). The is_relative_to guard ensures the resolved path cannot escape the designated public directory, effectively preventing path traversal. The subsequent existence check further validates the target. Thus the code includes the necessary safety checks, and the scanner’s finding is a false positive.

Impact

If the guard were missing, an attacker could read arbitrary files on the server, leading to data disclosure. However, the guard is present, so no impact is present.

Location

api/extra.py:221

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/path-injection

Evidence

  • source_excerpt lines 219‑224 show the guard if not file.is_relative_to(root) or not file.is_file(): raise HTTPException(404) which blocks traversal.

Recommended fix

No remediation needed; the existing is_relative_to check correctly mitigates path traversal. Ensure this pattern is retained for any similar file‑access endpoints.

Why this decision

Issue group ai-b3070a1396939b3b

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006AI discovery

64. Potential path traversal via unverified file name (CWE-22)

Problem

The endpoint @router.get("/files") builds a file path by joining the user‑supplied name with the public directory and then calling .resolve(). It attempts to prevent traversal by checking file.is_relative_to(FILES.resolve()) after resolution. Because Path.resolve() follows symlinks, an attacker could craft a filename that resolves to a location outside the intended directory via a symlink placed in FILES/public. The supplied code does not perform canonicalization before the join nor enforce a whitelist of allowed filenames, so the check may be bypassed on filesystems that allow such symlink tricks.

Impact

If an attacker can bypass the relative‑path check, they could read arbitrary files on the server, potentially exposing source code, configuration secrets, or other sensitive data.

Location

api/extra.py:210

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • api/extra.py:210 – endpoint definition @router.get("/files")
  • api/extra.py:212 – constructs file = (FILES / "public" / name).resolve()
  • api/extra.py:213 – checks if not file.is_relative_to(FILES.resolve()) or not file.is_file():

Limitations

  • No runtime probe or scanner evidence confirming that the check can be bypassed in this deployment.
  • No information about the presence of symlinks inside FILES/public or the filesystem behavior of resolve() on the target system.

Recommended fix

Validate the name parameter before joining it with the base directory. Options include:
* Reject any path containing ".." or path separators.
* Use a strict whitelist of allowed filenames.
* Perform the relative‑path check on the joined path before calling resolve(), or use os.path.normpath and compare the normalized path to the allowed directory.
* Ensure that the public directory does not contain symlinks that point outside the root, or explicitly resolve symlinks only after the check.
* Consider serving files via a dedicated static‑file handler that enforces these constraints automatically.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • source verification confirmed api/extra.py:210 (file_exists, line_range_valid, evidence_present_in_excerpt, candidate_context_snapshot_matches)

Evidence basis: source verified

Issue group ai-e358d3ef59fad665

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006AI discovery

66. Potential insecure token handling in refresh endpoint (CWE-613)

Problem

The /auth/refresh endpoint creates a new access token based solely on the Authorization header supplied by the caller. The code calls principal() on the header and then issues a fresh token with make_token(row) without any explicit verification that the request contains a dedicated refresh token, that the token is still valid, or that it has not been revoked. This pattern can allow an attacker who possesses a valid access token to repeatedly obtain new tokens, leading to token replay and insufficient session expiration.

Impact

An attacker who can capture or guess a valid access token could repeatedly call the refresh endpoint to obtain fresh tokens, extending the session indefinitely and bypassing any intended token lifetime controls. This undermines session security and may enable unauthorized access to protected resources.

Location

api/app.py:189

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • api/app.py:189 – @app.post("/auth/refresh") defines the refresh endpoint
  • api/app.py:191 – user = principal(authorization) uses the Authorization header without distinguishing token type
  • api/app.py:193 – row = conn.execute(... ) fetches user data based on the principal claim
  • api/app.py:196 – return {"access_token": make_token(row), "token_type": "bearer"} issues a new token unconditionally

Limitations

  • No evidence that a dedicated refresh token is stored, validated, or checked for expiration elsewhere in the codebase
  • principal() implementation is not shown, so it is unclear whether it differentiates access and refresh tokens
  • No independent scanner or runtime probe confirming that the endpoint can be abused to obtain unlimited tokens

Recommended fix

Implement proper refresh‑token handling: require a separate refresh token (e.g., in a secure HttpOnly cookie or request body), validate its signature, expiration, and revocation status, and ensure it is distinct from the access token. Only issue a new access token after successful verification of the refresh token. Consider adding rotation and revocation mechanisms for refresh tokens.

Issue group merged-56ecc860a2c48330a29a

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006DynamicCWE-693

69. Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set

Problem

The endpoint /comments returns an HTML response constructed from database content without setting a Content‑Security‑Policy header. The supplied source excerpt shows the response is built and returned directly, and the runtime probe confirms the endpoint is reachable via GET. No code is present that adds security‑related response headers, and the scanner flagged the missing CSP header. Because the evidence consists of a scanner finding and runtime observation but lacks a direct code reference that demonstrates the header is omitted, the finding meets the criteria for a likely vulnerability rather than a confirmed one.

Impact

Without a CSP header, browsers are free to execute any inline scripts or load resources from any origin referenced in the page. An attacker who can inject malicious content into the body field (e.g., via another vulnerable endpoint) could achieve stored cross‑site scripting (XSS), leading to session hijacking, credential theft, or other client‑side attacks.

Location

/comments

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Scanner finding: Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set (zap.10038) for path /comments
  • Runtime probe confirms GET request to /comments is reachable

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /comments · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Recorded output
cwe_id693
methodGET
parameter
scanner_rule10038
values_redactedyes
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium
confidencehigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • No source code fragment showing the absence of a CSP header or a header‑setting mechanism; the assessment relies on the scanner’s observation. Direct evidence that the application never adds the header (e.g., middleware configuration) is missing.

Recommended fix

Add a CSP header to the response, preferably using a middleware or response‑class that injects the header for all HTML responses. For example, in FastAPI you can use a custom Response or a middleware:
``python
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers["Content‑Security‑Policy"] = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'"
return response
`
Alternatively, set the header directly in show_comments` before returning the HTML.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /comments:? rules=10038

from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers['Content‑Security‑Policy'] = "default-src 'self'"
return response
`
Alternatively, set the header directly in view_home:
`python
return HTMLResponse(content, headers={"Content‑Security‑Policy": "default-src 'self'"})
``

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /view:? rules=10038

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

81. Wildcard CORS configuration allows any origin

Problem

The FastAPI application adds CORSMiddleware with allow_origins set to "*", which permits any origin to make cross‑origin requests. This configuration can expose the API to CSRF-like attacks or data leakage when combined with credentials. No evidence of a restrictive origin list or additional validation is present in the supplied excerpt.

Impact

An attacker from any domain can interact with the API, potentially stealing sensitive data or performing actions on behalf of authenticated users if credentials are included.

Location

api/app.py:26

Limitations

  • No runtime probe demonstrating that malicious origins can successfully interact with the API, and no evidence that additional origin checks are performed elsewhere in the code.

Recommended fix

Restrict allow_origins to a whitelist of trusted domains, or dynamically validate the Origin header. If credentials are required, ensure allow_credentials is only true for trusted origins. Example:
``python
app.add_middleware(
CORSMiddleware,
allow_origins=["https://example.com"],
allow_credentials=True,
allow_methods=["GET", "POST"],
allow_headers=["Authorization", "Content-Type"],
)
``

The /healthz endpoint returns a 200 response without the X-Content-Type-Options header, which can allow MIME type sniffing attacks. The scanner observed the missing header, but no additional evidence (e.g., runtime probe confirming exploitability or a guard that sets the header elsewhere) is provided.

Impact

Issue group merged-bbf1646deeb18d1e95db

LOW⚠️ warn · RG-006StaticCWE-939

87. Dynamic Urllib Use Detected

Problem

The code constructs a urllib.request.Request using a URL taken directly from the job payload (payload["target_url"]). No validation or sanitisation of this value is shown before it is passed to urllib.request.urlopen, which can handle arbitrary schemes (including file://). This creates a potential Server‑Side Request Forgery (SSRF) or local file read issue if an attacker can control the payload.

Impact

An attacker who can influence the "target_url" field could cause the worker to make arbitrary network requests or read local files, potentially exfiltrating data or accessing internal services.

Location

worker/worker.py:23

Detected by

  • semgrep-ce: dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence

  • source_excerpt lines 17‑23 show payload["target_url"] passed directly to urllib.request.Request without validation

Limitations

  • Missing evidence that the surrounding code performs any URL validation, scheme restriction, or other guard checks. No runtime probe confirming that malicious URLs can be supplied or that the request is blocked. Additional context (e.g., input sanitisation elsewhere) is required to confirm the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Validate and whitelist the scheme and host of "target_url" before using it. Reject or sanitise any URLs that are not explicitly allowed (e.g., only https:// with approved domains). Consider using a higher‑level HTTP client (e.g., requests) with built‑in timeout and redirect controls, and enforce strict URL parsing. If external requests are not required, remove the dynamic request entirely.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: low likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner semgrep-ce low at worker/worker.py:23 rules=dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence basis: ai assertion only

91. python-multipart: Python-Multipart: Negative Content-Length in parse_form buffers the entire body in memory

Problem

The scanner reports that python-multipart version 0.0.20 contains CVE-2026-53540, which can cause the entire request body to be buffered in memory when a negative Content‑Length header is present. The supplied requirements.txt confirms that this vulnerable version is declared as a dependency. However, no code or runtime evidence is provided that the application actually imports or uses python-multipart, nor that a negative Content‑Length request is processed. Without proof of an exploitable code path, the finding remains a potential issue rather than a confirmed vulnerability.

Impact

If an attacker can send a request with a crafted negative Content‑Length header and the application processes multipart data using the vulnerable library, the server may allocate unbounded memory, leading to denial‑of‑service or out‑of‑memory crashes.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-53540

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-53540 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.31.
  • requirements.txt lists python-multipart==0.0.20

Limitations

  • No evidence that the application imports or invokes python-multipart.
  • No runtime probe showing a request with a negative Content‑Length being processed.
  • No confirmation that the vulnerable code path is reachable in the deployed configuration.

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.31 or later, which resolves CVE‑2026‑53540. Verify that the updated version is used in the deployed environment and run dependency checks after the upgrade.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: low likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner trivy low at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-53540

1. python-multipart: Python-Multipart: Denial of Service via crafted form-urlencoded bodies

requirements.txt

Problem

The project’s requirements.txt pins python-multipart at version 0.0.20, which is known to contain CVE‑2026‑53539. The CVE describes a denial‑of‑service condition triggered by specially crafted form‑urlencoded bodies. No version‑upgrade or mitigation is present in the supplied dependency list, and the scanner evidence is of high severity, providing independent corroboration of the vulnerability.

Impact

An attacker can send a maliciously crafted request that causes the application to consume excessive resources or crash, leading to denial of service for legitimate users.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-53539

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-53539 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.30.

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.30 or later, which contains the fix for CVE‑2026‑53539. Update the requirements.txt accordingly and redeploy the application.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner trivy high at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-53539

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-051697f9b8567d596874

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

2. A note must belong to the order named in the URL

/orders/1/notes/2

Problem

The endpoint /orders/{order_id}/notes/{note_id} retrieves a note by its ID without verifying that the note's order_id matches the order_id supplied in the URL. The code executes a query SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s and returns the row if found, raising a 404 only when the note does not exist. No check is performed to ensure the note belongs to the requested order, allowing an attacker to access notes belonging to other orders by guessing note IDs. Runtime probing confirms the flaw: a GET request to /orders/1/notes/2 returned HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403/404, demonstrating unauthorized access.

Impact

An attacker can enumerate or read notes belonging to other users' orders, leading to confidentiality breaches and potential exposure of sensitive information across accounts.

Location

/orders/1/notes/2

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source code shows note fetched without order_id check (lines 543‑549).
  • Runtime probe fact: GET /orders/1/notes/2 returned status 200, contradicting expected 403/404.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.nested_note.1 · target /orders/1/notes/2 · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: GET /orders/1/notes/2violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output
actoruser_a
assertion_resultsfail
expected_statuses403, 404
methodGET
status200
step1
values_redactedyes
workflow_idnested_note
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Add a validation step that compares the order_id of the retrieved note with the order_id path parameter. If they differ, return a 403 Forbidden or 404 Not Found. Example:
``python
row = conn.execute("SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s", (note_id,)).fetchone()
if not row or row["order_id"] != order_id:
raise HTTPException(404, "Note not found")
return dict(row)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/1/notes/2:? rules=1

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

4. Insecure profile access allowing enumeration of other users' data

/profiles/{{other_id}}

Problem

The endpoint /profiles/{profile_id} authenticates the request via principal(authorization) but does not verify that the authenticated user is the owner of the requested profile before querying the database. The SQL query returns the full user record (including sensitive fields) for any profile_id supplied. Runtime probing confirms that a request for another user's profile returns HTTP 200, whereas the expected behavior is a 403 or 404.

Impact

An attacker can retrieve arbitrary users' personal information (username, email, SSN) by iterating profile_id values, leading to privacy breaches and potential identity theft.

Location

/profiles/{{other_id}}

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 2

  • Runtime probe fact: GET /profiles/{{other_id}} returned status 200 instead of expected 403/404

5. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

api/extra.py:119

Problem

The legacy_login endpoint constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided username and password into the query string using an f‑string. No parameterized query or input sanitization is performed, and there is no visible guard or validation step before the query is executed. This allows an attacker to inject arbitrary SQL via the username or password fields, leading to unauthorized data access or manipulation.

Impact

An attacker can perform SQL injection to retrieve, modify, or delete data from the users table, potentially compromising all user accounts and escalating privileges.

Location

api/extra.py:119

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • api/extra.py: line 117 builds query with f"SELECT id,username,role FROM users WHERE username='{body.username}' AND password='{body.password}'"
  • No input sanitization or parameterization is present before conn.execute(query)

Recommended fix

Replace the string interpolation with a parameterized query using the database library's placeholders (e.g., SELECT ... WHERE username=? AND password=?) and pass the user inputs as parameters. Additionally, hash passwords and compare hashes instead of storing plaintext passwords.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:119 rules=py/sql-injection

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

6. Payment replay vulnerability on order payment endpoint

/orders/{{order_id}}/pay

Problem

The pay_order endpoint updates the order status to 'paid' without verifying whether the order has already been paid. The runtime probe confirms that a second POST to the same endpoint returns HTTP 200 instead of an error (expected 400/409/422), demonstrating that the endpoint allows replay attacks.

Impact

An attacker can submit multiple payment requests for the same order, causing duplicate charges or fraudulent invoicing.

Location

/orders/{{order_id}}/pay

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-312CWE-359CWE-532

7. Clear-text logging of sensitive information

api/app.py:137

Problem

The code logs the application secret (APP_SECRET) in clear text via log.info("login user=%s app_secret=%s", username, os.environ.get("APP_SECRET", "lab-only")). No redaction or conditional guard is present before the logging statement, allowing the secret to be written to logs. The semgrep scanner flagged this line as a credential disclosure issue (rule python.lang.security.audit.logging.logger-credential-leak), providing independent corroboration of the vulnerability.

Impact

Exposing the application secret in logs can lead to credential leakage. An attacker with access to log files could retrieve the secret and potentially impersonate the application, access protected resources, or compromise the entire system.

Location

api/app.py:137

Detected by

  • codeql-cli + semgrep-ce: py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data, python-logger-credential-disclosure

Evidence

  • Source line 137 logs app_secret=%s with the value from os.environ.get("APP_SECRET", "lab-only")
  • Semgrep finding (python.lang.security.audit.logging.logger-credential-leak) flags the same line as a credential disclosure

Recommended fix

Remove the secret from the log statement or replace it with a placeholder. If logging is required for debugging, ensure that sensitive values are masked or omitted. Example:
``python
log.info("login user=%s", username) # Do not log APP_SECRET
``
Alternatively, use a dedicated secret‑masking logger or environment‑specific logging configuration that redacts secrets.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli,semgrep-ce high at api/app.py:137 rules=py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data|python-logger-credential-disclosure

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-3202e1e1074cfd44b9bf

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-606CWE-770

8. python-multipart: Denial of Service via excessive multipart part headers

requirements.txt

Problem

The project’s requirements.txt pins python-multipart at version 0.0.20, which is known to contain CVE‑2026‑42561. This vulnerability allows an attacker to craft a multipart request with a large number of part headers, causing the library to consume excessive memory and CPU, leading to a denial‑of‑service condition. No evidence of an upgrade or mitigation is present in the supplied files.

Impact

An attacker can cause the FastAPI service to become unresponsive or crash by sending specially crafted multipart requests, resulting in service outage and potential loss of availability for legitimate users.

Location

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-79CWE-116OWASP A03 Injection

17. Reflected server-side cross-site scripting

api/extra.py:145

Problem

The endpoint /catalog/search directly interpolates the user‑controlled query parameter q into an HTML response (<h1>Results for {q}</h1> and <p>{row['name']}</p>). No escaping or sanitisation is performed, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript that will be executed in the victim's browser when the page is rendered. The same unsanitised value is also used in a raw SQL string, indicating additional risk (SQL injection). The supplied source excerpt demonstrates the vulnerable code path, and the finding originates from a scanner, providing independent corroboration of the issue.

Impact

An attacker can execute arbitrary JavaScript in the context of the victim's browser, leading to session hijacking, credential theft, defacement, or further attacks against the user or the application.

Location

api/extra.py:145

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/reflective-xss

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows q interpolated into HTML without escaping (lines 142‑145).
  • Scanner‑generated finding flagged the endpoint as reflected XSS (high severity).

Recommended fix

Validate and sanitise the q parameter before using it in HTML output. Use a templating engine that auto‑escapes content or explicitly escape special characters (e.g., html.escape). Additionally, replace the string‑formatted SQL query with parameterised queries to prevent SQL injection. Example:
``python
@router.get("/catalog/search", response_class=HTMLResponse)
def catalog_search(q: str = ""):
with connection() as conn:
rows = conn.execute("SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s", (f"%{q}%",)).fetchall()
safe_q = html.escape(q)
return f"<h1>Results for {safe_q}</h1>" + "".join(f"<p>{html.escape(row['name'])}</p>" for row in rows)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:145 rules=py/reflective-xss

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Recommended fix

Add explicit validation to ensure the discount does not exceed an acceptable maximum (e.g., 100% or a business‑defined cap). Return a 4xx error when the supplied discount is out of bounds. Example:
``python
MAX_DISCOUNT = 50 # percent
if body.discount is not None:
if not (0 <= body.discount <= MAX_DISCOUNT):
raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Discount out of range")
percent = body.discount
else:
percent = coupon["percent"] if coupon else 0
``

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/{{order_id}}/apply-coupon:? rules=2

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

41. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

api/extra.py:144

Problem

The endpoint catalog_search constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided query parameter q into the statement using an f‑string. This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized, escaped, or parameterized before being sent to the database.

Impact

An attacker could supply crafted input (e.g., q=anything' OR '1'='1) to manipulate the SQL query, potentially retrieving all product names, extracting data, or executing arbitrary SQL commands depending on database permissions.

Location

api/extra.py:144

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt lines 141‑145 show the f‑string interpolation of q into the SQL query without sanitization.

Limitations

  • No independent runtime probe or additional scanner evidence confirming exploitation. A proof‑of‑concept request demonstrating the injection effect is not provided.

Recommended fix

Rewrite the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database driver, e.g., conn.execute("SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s", (f"%{q}%",)). Additionally, validate or whitelist allowed characters for q and consider limiting the length of the input.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:144 rules=py/sql-injection

42. Uncontrolled data used in path expression

api/extra.py:221

Problem

The endpoint constructs a file path from a user‑provided name but immediately resolves it and checks file.is_relative_to(root) and file.is_file(). The is_relative_to guard ensures the resolved path cannot escape the designated public directory, effectively preventing path traversal. The subsequent existence check further validates the target. Thus the code includes the necessary safety checks, and the scanner’s finding is a false positive.

Impact

If the guard were missing, an attacker could read arbitrary files on the server, leading to data disclosure. However, the guard is present, so no impact is present.

Location

api/extra.py:221

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/path-injection

Evidence

  • source_excerpt lines 219‑224 show the guard if not file.is_relative_to(root) or not file.is_file(): raise HTTPException(404) which blocks traversal.

Recommended fix

No remediation needed; the existing is_relative_to check correctly mitigates path traversal. Ensure this pattern is retained for any similar file‑access endpoints.

Issue group ai-b3070a1396939b3b

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006AI discovery

64. Potential path traversal via unverified file name (CWE-22)

api/extra.py:210

Problem

The endpoint @router.get("/files") builds a file path by joining the user‑supplied name with the public directory and then calling .resolve(). It attempts to prevent traversal by checking file.is_relative_to(FILES.resolve()) after resolution. Because Path.resolve() follows symlinks, an attacker could craft a filename that resolves to a location outside the intended directory via a symlink placed in FILES/public. The supplied code does not perform canonicalization before the join nor enforce a whitelist of allowed filenames, so the check may be bypassed on filesystems that allow such symlink tricks.

Impact

If an attacker can bypass the relative‑path check, they could read arbitrary files on the server, potentially exposing source code, configuration secrets, or other sensitive data.

Location

api/extra.py:210

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • api/extra.py:210 – endpoint definition @router.get("/files")
  • api/extra.py:212 – constructs file = (FILES / "public" / name).resolve()
  • api/extra.py:213 – checks if not file.is_relative_to(FILES.resolve()) or not file.is_file():

Limitations

  • No runtime probe or scanner evidence confirming that the check can be bypassed in this deployment.
  • No information about the presence of symlinks inside FILES/public or the filesystem behavior of resolve() on the target system.

Recommended fix

Validate the name parameter before joining it with the base directory. Options include:
* Reject any path containing ".." or path separators.
* Use a strict whitelist of allowed filenames.
* Perform the relative‑path check on the joined path before calling resolve(), or use os.path.normpath and compare the normalized path to the allowed directory.
* Ensure that the public directory does not contain symlinks that point outside the root, or explicitly resolve symlinks only after the check.
* Consider serving files via a dedicated static‑file handler that enforces these constraints automatically.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • source verification confirmed api/extra.py:210 (file_exists, line_range_valid, evidence_present_in_excerpt, candidate_context_snapshot_matches)

Issue group ai-e358d3ef59fad665

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006AI discovery

66. Potential insecure token handling in refresh endpoint (CWE-613)

api/app.py:189

Problem

The /auth/refresh endpoint creates a new access token based solely on the Authorization header supplied by the caller. The code calls principal() on the header and then issues a fresh token with make_token(row) without any explicit verification that the request contains a dedicated refresh token, that the token is still valid, or that it has not been revoked. This pattern can allow an attacker who possesses a valid access token to repeatedly obtain new tokens, leading to token replay and insufficient session expiration.

Impact

An attacker who can capture or guess a valid access token could repeatedly call the refresh endpoint to obtain fresh tokens, extending the session indefinitely and bypassing any intended token lifetime controls. This undermines session security and may enable unauthorized access to protected resources.

Location

api/app.py:189

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

  • api/app.py:189 – @app.post("/auth/refresh") defines the refresh endpoint
  • api/app.py:191 – user = principal(authorization) uses the Authorization header without distinguishing token type
  • api/app.py:193 – row = conn.execute(... ) fetches user data based on the principal claim
  • api/app.py:196 – return {"access_token": make_token(row), "token_type": "bearer"} issues a new token unconditionally

Limitations

  • No evidence that a dedicated refresh token is stored, validated, or checked for expiration elsewhere in the codebase
  • principal() implementation is not shown, so it is unclear whether it differentiates access and refresh tokens
  • No independent scanner or runtime probe confirming that the endpoint can be abused to obtain unlimited tokens

Recommended fix

Implement proper refresh‑token handling: require a separate refresh token (e.g., in a secure HttpOnly cookie or request body), validate its signature, expiration, and revocation status, and ensure it is distinct from the access token. Issue group merged-56ecc860a2c48330a29a

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006DynamicCWE-693

69. Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set

/comments

Problem

The endpoint /comments returns an HTML response constructed from database content without setting a Content‑Security‑Policy header. The supplied source excerpt shows the response is built and returned directly, and the runtime probe confirms the endpoint is reachable via GET. No code is present that adds security‑related response headers, and the scanner flagged the missing CSP header. Because the evidence consists of a scanner finding and runtime observation but lacks a direct code reference that demonstrates the header is omitted, the finding meets the criteria for a likely vulnerability rather than a confirmed one.

Impact

Without a CSP header, browsers are free to execute any inline scripts or load resources from any origin referenced in the page. An attacker who can inject malicious content into the body field (e.g., via another vulnerable endpoint) could achieve stored cross‑site scripting (XSS), leading to session hijacking, credential theft, or other client‑side attacks.

Location

/comments

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Scanner finding: Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set (zap.10038) for path /comments
  • Runtime probe confirms GET request to /comments is reachable

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /comments · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Recorded output
cwe_id693
methodGET
parameter
scanner_rule10038
values_redactedyes
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium
confidencehigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • No source code fragment showing the absence of a CSP header or a header‑setting mechanism; the assessment relies on the scanner’s observation. Direct evidence that the application never adds the header (e.g., middleware configuration) is missing.

Recommended fix

Add a CSP header to the response, preferably using a middleware or response‑class that injects the header for all HTML responses. For example, in FastAPI you can use a custom Response or a middleware:
``python
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers["Content‑Security‑Policy"] = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'"
return response
`
Alternatively, set the header directly in show_comments` before returning the HTML.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /comments:? rules=10038

No guard or middleware is visible in the supplied code that would add such a header. The runtime probe only records the request method and that values were redacted; it does not confirm the presence or absence of the CSP header.

Impact

Without a CSP header, browsers are free to load scripts, styles, and other resources from any origin, increasing the risk of cross‑site scripting (XSS) and data‑injection attacks.

Location

/view

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows HTMLResponse returned without CSP header
  • Scanner finding reports CSP header not set for /view

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /view · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • No runtime probe confirming that the CSP header is absent in actual responses; only method and redacted values are recorded. Additional evidence such as an HTTP response capture showing missing CSP header would be needed to confirm the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Add a CSP header to the response, either by setting it explicitly in the view or by configuring a security middleware that injects the header for all responses. Example (FastAPI):
``python
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers['Content‑Security‑Policy'] = "default-src 'self'"
return response
`
Alternatively, set the header directly in view_home:
`python
return HTMLResponse(content, headers={"Content‑Security‑Policy": "default-src 'self'"})
``

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /view:? rules=10038

No guard (auth check, signature verification, size limit, or rate‑limit) is visible in the supplied excerpt.

Impact

An attacker can send arbitrary payloads to the endpoint, causing uncontrolled data insertion, potential log or SQL injection, database bloat, or abuse of downstream processes that rely on the stored data. 85. X-Content-Type-Options Header Missing

/healthz

Problem

The /healthz endpoint returns a JSON response but the source code does not show any middleware or response header configuration that adds the X-Content-Type-Options header. The scanner observed the header is absent, indicating a potential MIME‑type sniffing risk.

Impact

An attacker could influence how browsers interpret the response content, possibly leading to content‑type confusion attacks such as MIME sniffing, which can facilitate cross‑site scripting or data leakage in certain contexts.

Location

/healthz

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10021

Evidence

  • Scanner runtime probe (zap.10021) reported missing X-Content-Type-Options header on GET /healthz

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Additional evidence such as the full application startup configuration or response‑header middleware definitions is needed.

Recommended fix

Configure the FastAPI application to include the X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff header for all responses, e.g., by adding a middleware that sets this header globally or specifically for the /healthz route.

Issue group merged-bbf1646deeb18d1e95db

LOW⚠️ warn · RG-006StaticCWE-939

87. Dynamic Urllib Use Detected

worker/worker.py:23

Problem

The code constructs a urllib.request.Request using a URL taken directly from the job payload (payload["target_url"]). No validation or sanitisation of this value is shown before it is passed to urllib.request.urlopen, which can handle arbitrary schemes (including file://). This creates a potential Server‑Side Request Forgery (SSRF) or local file read issue if an attacker can control the payload.

Impact

An attacker who can influence the "target_url" field could cause the worker to make arbitrary network requests or read local files, potentially exfiltrating data or accessing internal services.

Location

worker/worker.py:23

Detected by

  • semgrep-ce: dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence

  • source_excerpt lines 17‑23 show payload["target_url"] passed directly to urllib.request.Request without validation

Limitations

  • Missing evidence that the surrounding code performs any URL validation, scheme restriction, or other guard checks. No runtime probe confirming that malicious URLs can be supplied or that the request is blocked. Additional context (e.g., input sanitisation elsewhere) is required to confirm the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Validate and whitelist the scheme and host of "target_url" before using it. Reject or sanitise any URLs that are not explicitly allowed (e.g., only https:// with approved domains). Consider using a higher‑level HTTP client (e.g., requests) with built‑in timeout and redirect controls, and enforce strict URL parsing. If external requests are not required, remove the dynamic request entirely.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: low likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner semgrep-ce low at worker/worker.py:23 rules=dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence basis: ai assertion only

89. X-Content-Type-Options Header Missing

/comments

Problem

The scanner reports that the HTTP response for GET /comments does not include the X-Content-Type-Options header. The provided source code shows the endpoint returns an HTMLResponse but does not set any security headers. No evidence of a middleware or framework configuration that adds this header is present in the supplied context.

Impact

Without the X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff header, browsers may attempt to sniff the MIME type of the response, potentially allowing content‑type confusion attacks such as cross‑site scripting (XSS) if an attacker can influence the response body.

Location

values_redactedyes

vulnerableyes

Issue group merged-e76230cbef200116e4ae

LOW⚠️ warn · RG-006StaticCWE-1284

91. python-multipart: Python-Multipart: Negative Content-Length in parse_form buffers the entire body in memory

requirements.txt

Problem

The scanner reports that python-multipart version 0.0.20 contains CVE-2026-53540, which can cause the entire request body to be buffered in memory when a negative Content‑Length header is present. The supplied requirements.txt confirms that this vulnerable version is declared as a dependency. However, no code or runtime evidence is provided that the application actually imports or uses python-multipart, nor that a negative Content‑Length request is processed. Without proof of an exploitable code path, the finding remains a potential issue rather than a confirmed vulnerability.

Impact

If an attacker can send a request with a crafted negative Content‑Length header and the application processes multipart data using the vulnerable library, the server may allocate unbounded memory, leading to denial‑of‑service or out‑of‑memory crashes.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-53540

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-53540 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.31.
  • requirements.txt lists python-multipart==0.0.20

Limitations

  • No evidence that the application imports or invokes python-multipart.
  • No runtime probe showing a request with a negative Content‑Length being processed.
  • No confirmation that the vulnerable code path is reachable in the deployed configuration.

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.31 or later, which resolves CVE‑2026‑53540. Verify that the updated version is used in the deployed environment and run dependency checks after the upgrade.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: low likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner trivy low at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-53540

Evidence basis: ai assertion only

92. No HEALTHCHECK defined

Dockerfile.worker

Problem

The Dockerfile for the worker service does not contain a HEALTHCHECK instruction. While the absence of a health check can reduce observability and automatic recovery capabilities, the supplied artifact does not demonstrate any concrete impact or exploitation path. No runtime probe or additional scanner evidence confirms that this omission leads to a vulnerability in the deployed environment.

Impact

Without a HEALTHCHECK, orchestration platforms cannot automatically detect and restart unhealthy containers, potentially leading to prolonged service outages.

Location

Dockerfile.worker

Detected by

  • trivy: DS-0026

Evidence

  • Dockerfile excerpt shows no HEALTHCHECK line

Limitations

  • No runtime_probe_facts demonstrating failed health checks or orchestration response
  • No evidence that the container is deployed in an environment where health checks are required
  • No scanner evidence of exploitation or impact from the missing health check

Recommended fix

Add an appropriate HEALTHCHECK instruction to the Dockerfile, e.g.,

HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=5s --retries=3 CMD curl -f http://localhost/health || exit 1

Issue group merged-df83daf10f817c7aa965

MEDIUM✅ allow · RG-004StaticCWE-117

93. Log Injection

api/app.py:137

Problem

The log statement uses the logger's built‑in formatting with a static format string and passes the user‑provided username as a separate argument (log.info("login user=%s app_secret=%s", username, os.environ.get(...))). This pattern prevents format string injection because the logger does not interpret the user data as a format specifier. No evidence of uncontrolled format strings or direct string concatenation with user input is present, so the finding does not constitute a log injection vulnerability.

Impact

None – the logging call safely handles user input and does not allow injection of malicious log content.

Location

api/app.py:137

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/log-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows safe logger usage with a constant format string and separate arguments for user‑provided values.

Recommended fix

No remediation required for this specific log statement; continue using structured logging with format arguments.

Why this decision

  • RG-004: classified as a false positive; no high/critical scanner finding contradicts it.
  • scanner codeql-cli medium at api/app.py:137 rules=py/log-injection

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Scan pipeline

Which agents and tests ran, and how the results became a decision.

Judges every issue group and writes the verdict

  • 96 of 96 issue groups reviewed

  • Provider returned an unknown or missing issue group ID; it was ignored.

Scanners and tests4 of 4 ran

  • Dynamic tests4/4
  • Baseline web probes17 probe(s) · 3 finding(s)
  • OWASP ZAPcompleted · zap-active · 14 alert(s)
  • Business workflowscompleted · 40 executed · 20 violations
  • Browser crawlcompleted · 1 page(s) visited

Pipeline

  1. 64
    raw scanner resultsStatic and dynamic findings before de-duplication.
  2. 63
    root-cause records1 duplicate(s) merged: same vulnerability family, file and overlapping lines. Nothing is discarded.
  3. 96
    issue groups reviewedIncludes 33 group(s) that came only from AI candidates.
  4. 19
    blocking release73 warn · 4 allow. Decided by named release-gate rules, never by the AI description alone.

Known gaps (7)

Each gap lowers confidence in a clean result for that area.

  • A negative result means only that this probe did not observe the behavior.
  • A violation is an observed contract mismatch requiring security review.
  • Baseline checks use bounded HTTP GET observations for an authorized target; declared paths must be safe to visit.
  • Browser crawl is same-origin and GET-only; forms, clicks, OAuth/MFA and destructive workflows were not submitted.
  • Coverage applies only to declared assertions, accounts and workflows.
  • Fresh stack resets the run; workflows must create independent test data. No per-step database reset is performed.
  • This baseline phase only makes bounded HTTP GET observations; browser crawling, ZAP and account workflows are separate phases, reported only when they ran.

How to read this report

Observation

A raw scanner or DAST signal before review.

Issue group & verdict

Correlated evidence assessed by the Review Agent. A model assessment, not independent exploit proof.

Found by

Static = scanners reading code, dependencies, secrets and configuration. Dynamic = probes sent to the running app. AI discovery = candidates the AI agent found by reading source.

Policy decision

A release-gate outcome from a named rule. Never add it to the vulnerability count.

Coverage gap

No finding in an untested area does not mean it passed.
